Organism: Homo sapiens
Review Status: COMPLETE
📝 Provide Detailed Feedback

Gene Description

CDC20 (p55CDC) is the mitotic coactivator of the anaphase-promoting complex/cyclosome (APC/C), the multisubunit cullin-RING E3 ubiquitin ligase that drives the metaphase-to-anaphase transition and mitotic exit. CDC20 has no catalytic activity of its own: its N-terminal C-box, KILR motif and C-terminal IR tail dock it onto the mitotically phosphorylated APC/C (APC8/CDC23 and APC3/CDC27), where it relieves APC1 auto-inhibition, enhances E2 recruitment and, through its C-terminal WD40 beta-propeller, recognises D-box, KEN-box and ABBA degrons on substrates. APC/C-CDC20 polyubiquitinates securin (PTTG1) and cyclin B1, releasing separase to cleave cohesin and inactivating CDK1, and also targets cyclin A, NEK2A, KIF18A, BUBR1, USP22, FBXO31 and AXIN2. CDC20 is the direct target of the spindle assembly checkpoint: unattached kinetochores catalyse its incorporation into the mitotic checkpoint complex (BUBR1-BUB3-CDC20-MAD2), in which MAD2 and BUBR1 occupy its degron receptor sites and block E2 access so that APC/C stays inactive until all chromosomes are bioriented; APC/C-mediated ubiquitination of MCC-bound CDC20, opposed by USP44 and promoted by p31comet, then dissolves the MCC and reactivates the ligase. CDC20 is synthesised from G1/S, peaks in mitosis, is regulated by BUB1 phosphorylation and SIRT2 deacetylation, and is degraded at the M/G1 transition when CDH1/FZR1 takes over APC/C activation. It localises to kinetochores, spindle poles/centrosomes and the mitotic cytosol. In oocytes, APC/C-CDC20 executes homologue disjunction at the meiosis I metaphase/anaphase transition, and biallelic CDC20 loss-of-function variants cause oocyte maturation arrest and female infertility (OZEMA14). In rodent postmitotic neurons a centrosomal Cdc20-APC pool additionally regulates dendrite morphogenesis and presynaptic differentiation.

Existing Annotations Review

GO Term Evidence Action Reason
GO:0000776 kinetochore
IEA
GO_REF:0000107 		ACCEPT
Summary: CDC20 localises to kinetochores, where the spindle assembly checkpoint catalyses its incorporation into the MCC; kinetochore localisation of human CDC20 is shown directly in oocytes, and GFP-Cdc20 turns over dynamically at unattached kinetochores in living cells.
Reason: Kinetochore is a genuine site of CDC20 action (MCC assembly). The mouse-derived transfer is corroborated by human CDC20 constructs localising to kinetochores in oocytes (PMID:32666501), by live-cell FRAP at unattached kinetochores (PMID:15182668) and by UniProt subcellular location. Accepted as a core location.
Supporting Evidence:
PMID:32666501
CDC20 showed normal kinetochore localization as the wild-type
PMID:15182668
GFP-Cdc20 and GFP-BubR1 exhibit biphasic exponential kinetics at unattached kinetochores

IEA
GO_REF:0000044 		KEEP AS NON CORE
Summary: UniProt subcellular-location mapping: CDC20 is found at spindle poles/centrosomes in mitotic cells (UniProt cites PubMed:20034488, the NEK2 study, not cached).
Reason: Spindle-pole association is a documented but minor localisation; the functional sites of CDC20 are the APC/C and MCC assemblies in the mitotic cytosol and at kinetochores. Kept as a non-core cellular-component annotation.

IPI
PMID:10700282
Structure of the Mad2 spindle assembly checkpoint protein an... 		MODIFY
Summary: IPI with MAD2L1 (UniProtKB:Q13257). NMR structure of MAD2 and mapping of its binding site to a 40-residue segment of CDC20 N-terminal to the WD40 repeats.
Reason: Bare protein binding carries no functional information. The paper characterises the direct MAD2-CDC20 interaction that assembles the CDC20-MAD2 subcomplex of the mitotic checkpoint complex, so the informative annotation is membership of that subcomplex (GO:1990333), the same replacement used in the MAD2L1 review; the replacement is a cellular-component term because CDC20 is the inhibited target in this pair and no molecular-function term describes being sequestered.
Proposed replacements: mitotic checkpoint complex, CDC20-MAD2 subcomplex

The paper characterises the direct MAD2-CDC20 interaction that assembles the CDC20-MAD2 subcomplex of the mitotic checkpoint complex, so the informative annotation is membership of that subcomplex (GO:1990333), the same replacement used in the MAD2L1 review; the replacement is a cellular-component term because CDC20 is the inhibited target in this pair and no molecular-function term describes being sequestered.
Proposed replacements: mitotic checkpoint complex, CDC20-MAD2 subcomplex

Reason: High-throughput interactome dataset; the CDC20-CDC27 co-purification is expected (both are constituents of the APC/C-MCC assembly) and is already captured by the specific complex and APC/C-binding annotations. A bare protein-binding row from a screen adds no functional information, so it is removed; removal does not mean the interaction is false.

GO:0005680 anaphase-promoting complex
IBA
GO_REF:0000033 		ACCEPT
Summary: CDC20 is the mitotic coactivator subunit of the APC/C: it docks on the phosphorylated APC/C through its C-box (APC8/CDC23) and IR tail (APC3/CDC27) to form the substrate-recognising, catalytically competent APC/C-CDC20.
Reason: PAINT node PTN000460086 places the CDC20/CDH1 family as APC/C coactivator subunits, seeded by human CDC20/FZR1, fly fzy and budding-yeast CDC20/CDH1. Cryo-EM of human APC/C-CDC20 and APC/C-MCC confirms CDC20 as an integral, if cell-cycle-regulated, subunit of the active ligase. Accepted as the core complex.
Supporting Evidence:
PMID:27509861
Degron-like sequences of the MCC subunit BubR1 block degron recognition sites on Cdc20, the APC/C coactivator subunit responsible for substrate interactions
PMID:27120157
Hyperphosphorylation of APC/C subunits, notably Apc1 and Apc3, is required for Cdc20 to activate the APC/C
GO:0005680 anaphase-promoting complex
IDA
PMID:11459825
Inhibition of Cdh1-APC by the MAD2-related protein MAD2L2: a... 		ACCEPT
Summary: Kirschner-lab study of MAD2L2 inhibition of CDH1-APC, in which APC bound to CDC20 or CDH1 is characterised biochemically (colocalizes_with qualifier).
Reason: The paper works with CDC20-APC and CDH1-APC complexes and the curator recorded only the weaker colocalizes_with claim. Consistent with CDC20's established role as the APC/C coactivator; accepted.
Supporting Evidence:
PMID:11459825

IDA
PMID:14743218
The tumour suppressor RASSF1A regulates mitosis by inhibitin... 		ACCEPT
Summary: RASSF1A study: CDC20 is characterised as the activator of the APC and the APC-CDC20 complex is the target of RASSF1A inhibition.
Reason: Direct human-cell evidence that CDC20 assembles with the APC/C as its activating subunit; this is the core complex and the GO-CAM 6348a65d00002236 uses this reference for CDC20's APC/C membership. Accepted.

PMID:14743218

NAS
PMID:27120157
Molecular mechanism of APC/C activation by mitotic phosphory... 		ACCEPT
Summary: Cryo-EM of phosphorylated human APC/C-CDC20: hyperphosphorylation of Apc1/Apc3 relieves an Apc1 auto-inhibitory segment that otherwise blocks the C-box binding site, allowing CDC20 engagement.
Reason: Structural definition of the CDC20-bound APC/C; core complex. Accepted.

PMID:27120157

PMID:27120157

GO:0005813 centrosome
IEA
GO_REF:0000120 		KEEP AS NON CORE
Summary: Combined IEA (UniProt subcellular location plus mouse/rat ortholog transfer): CDC20 at the centrosome; in rodent neurons a centrosomal Cdc20-APC pool regulates dendrite morphogenesis.
Reason: Centrosome localisation is reported (UniProt, PubMed:20034488) and underlies the rodent neuronal Cdc20-APC pathway, but it is not the site of the core mitotic function. Kept as non-core.
GO:0005819 spindle
TAS
PMID:7513050
A novel mammalian protein, p55CDC, present in dividing cells... 		KEEP AS NON CORE
Summary: The 1994 p55CDC discovery paper; its spindle statement is an inference from homology to yeast Cdc20/Cdc4, which were 'proposed to be involved in the formation of a functional bipolar mitotic spindle'.
Reason: The cited source does not localise p55CDC to the spindle experimentally, so the TAS is weakly grounded; nevertheless later work places CDC20 at spindle poles and kinetochores (UniProt; PMID:15182668), and the mitotic spindle is used for the CDC20/USP44 GO-CAM 6348a65d00002236. Kept as a non-core location rather than removed.
Supporting Evidence:
PMID:7513050
the highest degree of homology in these repeats was found with the Saccharomyces cerevisiae Cdc20 and Cdc4 proteins, which have been proposed to be involved in the formation of a functional bipolar mitotic spindle in yeast cells
GO:0005829 cytosol
TAS
Reactome:R-HSA-141409 		ACCEPT
Summary: Reactome compartment assignment (cytosol) for 'Mad1 binds kinetochore', a reaction in which CDC20 is present only as a constituent of a larger kinetochore/spindle or cohesin-regulatory assembly.
Reason: The compartment claim is the same as for the APC/C-CDC20 and MCC reactions and is correct: CDC20 acts in the mitotic cytosol after nuclear-envelope breakdown. This particular Reactome reaction does not involve CDC20's own activity, so the row is redundant with the other cytosol rows, but the location itself is core and is accepted for consistency.

Reactome:R-HSA-141422 		ACCEPT

Core Functions

Ubiquitin ligase activator of the anaphase-promoting complex/cyclosome: CDC20 docks on the mitotically phosphorylated APC/C via its C-box, KILR motif and IR tail, relieves Apc1 auto-inhibition and enhances E2 affinity, converting the APC/C into the active APC/C-CDC20 ligase that polyubiquitinates securin and cyclin B1 to trigger sister-chromatid separation and CDK1 inactivation at the metaphase/anaphase transition.

anaphase-promoting complex-dependent catabolic process positive regulation of mitotic metaphase/anaphase transition metaphase/anaphase transition of mitotic cell cycle
Cellular Locations:
cytosol
In Complex:
anaphase-promoting complex

  • PMID:27120157
    Hyperphosphorylation of APC/C subunits, notably Apc1 and Apc3, is required for Cdc20 to activate the APC/C
  • PMID:9637688
    an inactive hMAD2-CDC20-APC ternary complex present at metaphase, a CDC20-APC binary complex active in degrading specific substrates at anaphase, and a CDH1-APC complex active later in mitosis and in G1

Substrate adaptor of the APC/C: the CDC20 WD40 beta-propeller recognises D-box, KEN-box and ABBA degrons on substrates (securin, cyclin B1, cyclin A, NEK2A, KIF18A, BUBR1, USP22, FBXO31, AXIN2) and presents them to the APC2-APC11 catalytic module for polyubiquitination and proteasomal degradation.

Inhibited effector of the spindle assembly checkpoint: at unattached kinetochores CDC20 is captured by C-MAD2 and BUBR1-BUB3 into the mitotic checkpoint complex, which blocks its degron receptor sites and E2 access and can also inhibit a second APC/C-bound CDC20, holding APC/C inactive until chromosome biorientation; APC/C-mediated ubiquitination of MCC-bound CDC20 then reactivates the ligase.

Deep research report for human CDC20 (falcon / Edison Scientific Literature)
  • CDC20 is a non-enzymatic WD40 coactivator and substrate receptor of the APC/C; APC/C, not CDC20, is the E3 ligase.
  • CDC20 is both the APC/C activator after correct attachment and the inhibited target sequestered in the MCC while kinetochores are unattached.

Suggested Questions for Experts

Q: Should GO represent CDC20's two coactivator facets with both GO:1990757 (ubiquitin ligase activator activity) and GO:1990756 (ubiquitin-like ligase-substrate adaptor activity), as the current IBA and IDA rows do, or is one term preferred for APC/C coactivators, given that the human GO-CAMs use GO:0010997 for the same node?

Q: Which molecular-function or component term best represents CDC20 as the inhibited target of the spindle assembly checkpoint, given that MCC membership (GO:0033597, GO:1990333) is the only informative replacement for the many MAD2L1/BUB1B protein-binding rows?

Q: Should the rodent neuronal Cdc20-APC roles (dendrite morphogenesis, presynaptic differentiation, synapse maturation) remain on human CDC20 as ISS/IEA transfers, given the absence of human evidence and their taxon-restricted physiological context?

Suggested Experiments

Experiment: Complement CRISPR-engineered RNAi-sensitive human cells with CRY-box, ABBA-site and BUBR1-interface CDC20 mutants; quantify MCC formation by co-IP, kinetochore localisation by live imaging, and APC/C-CDC20 inhibition in reconstituted cyclin B1 ubiquitination assays.

Hypothesis: The CDC20 CRY box and ABBA-binding surface are required for MCC assembly and kinetochore recruitment independently of CDC20's own degradation.

Type: mutational rescue and reconstituted ubiquitination assay

Experiment: Purify recombinant variant CDC20 proteins and compare APC/C binding, securin/cyclin B1 ubiquitination kinetics and MCC incorporation with wild type; complement Cdc20-depleted mouse oocytes and score meiosis I timing.

Hypothesis: Human OZEMA14 missense variants (p.Tyr228Cys, p.Arg322Gln, p.Leu439Arg) impair APC/C activation or degron recognition rather than kinetochore localisation.

Type: biochemical reconstitution and oocyte complementation

The research report should be a detailed narrative explaining the function, biological processes, and localization of the gene product. Citations should be given for all claims.

You should prioritize authoritative reviews and primary scientific literature when conducting research. You can supplement
this with annotations you find in gene/protein databases, but these can be outdated or inaccurate.

We are specifically interested in the primary function of the gene - for enzymes, what reaction is catalyzed, and what is the substrate specificity? For transporters, what is the substrate? For structural proteins or adapters, what is the broader structural role? For signaling molecules, what is the role in the pathway.

We are interested in where in or outside the cell the gene product carries out its function.

We are also interested in the signaling or biochemical pathways in which the gene functions. We are less interested in broad pleiotropic effects, except where these elucidate the precise role.

Include evidence where possible. We are interested in both experimental evidence as well as inference from structure, evolution, or bioinformatic analysis. Precise studies should be prioritized over high-throughput, where available.

Human CDC20 (UniProt Q12834): Functional-Annotation Research Report

Executive summary

The requested target is unambiguously human CDC20, UniProt Q12834, also called cell division cycle protein 20 homolog or p55CDC. It is distinct from CDC20B and from the related APC/C coactivator FZR1/CDH1. Open Targets maps human CDC20 to ENSG00000117399, while separately identifying CDC20B and FZR1, resolving the main symbol-confusion risk (OpenTargets Search: -CDC20).

CDC20 is not an enzyme by itself. Its primary function is to act as a WD40-repeat substrate receptor and allosteric coactivator for the anaphase-promoting complex/cyclosome, APC/C, a multisubunit RING-type E3 ubiquitin ligase. APC/C–CDC20 recognizes mitotic proteins bearing D-box, KEN-box, and related degrons and directs their polyubiquitylation. Destruction of securin activates separase and cohesin cleavage; destruction of cyclin B1 lowers CDK1 activity and enables mitotic exit. Before all chromosomes are correctly attached to spindle microtubules, the spindle-assembly checkpoint instead incorporates CDC20 into the inhibitory MAD2–BUBR1–BUB3 mitotic checkpoint complex (MCC). Thus, CDC20 is the molecular switch coupling chromosome attachment to irreversible anaphase onset (yamano2019apcccurrentunderstanding pages 3-5, liu2020theinteractionprofile pages 1-8, liu2020theinteractionprofile pages 12-16).

Topic Established finding Key molecular details Evidence type/strength
Identity and architecture The target is human CDC20 (UniProt Q12834; cell division cycle protein 20 homolog), distinct from CDC20B and the related APC/C coactivator FZR1/CDH1. Human CDC20 has an N-terminal regulatory region (approximately residues 1–173) containing BM1, KILR, C-box, KEN-box and CRY-box elements, followed by a C-terminal WD40 β-propeller (approximately residues 174–470), matching the CDC20/Fizzy WD-repeat family. Strong: curated human target identity plus human-cell structural–functional mapping (OpenTargets Search: -CDC20, zhang2024functionalanalysisof pages 1-2)
Primary molecular function CDC20 is a nonenzymatic coactivator and substrate receptor for the anaphase-promoting complex/cyclosome (APC/C); APC/C—not CDC20—is the E3 ubiquitin ligase. CDC20’s C-box and C-terminal IR motif engage/activate APC/C, while its WD40 propeller recruits selected substrates. APC/C uses UBE2C/UBE2D to initiate ubiquitination and UBE2S to extend predominantly K11-linked chains. Strong: structural, reconstituted biochemical and review-level synthesis (yamano2019apcccurrentunderstanding pages 5-6, yamano2019apcccurrentunderstanding pages 3-5)
Substrate recognition CDC20 recognizes short APC/C degrons, with combinatorial degron content and accessibility determining substrate timing and affinity. Distinct WD40 surfaces bind the D-box (typically RxxLxxxxN) and KEN-box (KENxxxN); the ABBA motif (Fx[ILV][FY]x[DE]) supplies an additional CDC20-binding interaction. Strong: human structural evidence and biochemical degron studies (cosma2022biochemicalbiophysicaland pages 14-15, cosma2022biochemicalbiophysicaland pages 13-14, yamano2019apcccurrentunderstanding pages 5-6)
Securin and cyclin B outcomes APC/C–CDC20 polyubiquitinates securin and cyclin B1 at the metaphase–anaphase transition, targeting them for proteasomal degradation. Securin loss releases separase to cleave cohesin and separate sister chromatids; cyclin B1 loss lowers CDK1 activity and drives mitotic exit. CDC20 is subsequently replaced functionally by CDH1 during late mitosis/G1. Strong: established biochemical and cell-cycle mechanism (yamano2019apcccurrentunderstanding pages 3-5, liu2020theinteractionprofile pages 1-8, liu2020theinteractionprofile pages 12-16)
Spindle-assembly checkpoint CDC20 has a dual role: it activates APC/C after correct chromosome attachment but is incorporated into the inhibitory mitotic checkpoint complex (MCC) while unattached or improperly attached kinetochores persist. MCC comprises CDC20, MAD2, BUBR1 and BUB3. MCC-bound CDC20 and an APC/C-associated second CDC20 generate an inhibited APC/C–MCC assembly, preventing premature securin/cyclin-B destruction. Strong: structural, biochemical and genetic evidence (cosma2022biochemicalbiophysicaland pages 14-15, cosma2022biochemicalbiophysicaland pages 8-10)
Localization and spatial action CDC20 acts in the mitotic compartment where it engages kinetochores, checkpoint proteins and APC/C; a simple fixed “nuclear” or “cytoplasmic” localization is inadequate because nuclear-envelope breakdown reorganizes this space. Human-cell immunofluorescence found kinetochore localization of tested CDC20 constructs except a mutant lacking the ABBA-binding region. HeLa-cell proximity assays detected cell-cycle-dependent APC/C engagement, with APC8–CDC20 proximity peaking in prometaphase and APC3–CDC20 proximity in metaphase. Direct evidence for stable human CDC20 localization along spindle microtubules is less secure. Moderate-to-strong for kinetochore/APC-C engagement; limited for broad spindle, nuclear or cytosolic assignments (zhang2024functionalanalysisof pages 3-5, liu2020theinteractionprofile pages 47-50, cosma2022biochemicalbiophysicaland pages 10-11)
2024 CRY-box advance A 2024 human-cell study showed that the conserved CRY box is not merely a CDC20 degradation signal: it is essential for robust spindle-checkpoint signaling. CRISPR-engineered, RNAi-sensitive HeLa cells complemented with CDC20 mutants showed that the CRY box promotes MCC formation and stabilizes MCC association with APC/C; its impairment weakened checkpoint-dependent inhibition. Strong recent mechanistic evidence: genetic complementation, live-cell phenotyping, co-immunoprecipitation and reconstituted ubiquitination assays (zhang2024functionalanalysisof pages 11-11, zhang2024functionalanalysisof pages 1-2, zhang2024functionalanalysisof pages 7-8)
Disease and translation Biallelic CDC20 dysfunction is associated with oocyte-maturation defects/female infertility, whereas elevated CDC20 expression is repeatedly associated with proliferative cancers and adverse features. These findings support biomarker and therapeutic-target research but do not establish CDC20 overexpression as an independent driver in every tumor type. 2024 studies linked CDC20 to breast- and lung-cancer outcomes and reported growth inhibition after knockdown; a bladder cohort found higher expression in high-grade tumors but no significant overall-survival association. Small molecules such as Apcin or proTAME and CDC20 knockdown remain preclinical strategies. No approved CDC20-directed therapy or relevant CDC20-directed clinical trial was identified. Human genetic evidence for infertility; mostly retrospective/bioinformatic cancer associations plus cell-line/xenograft experiments for oncology translation (OpenTargets Search: -CDC20, miao2024basedonmachine pages 7-11, messeha2024prognosticandtherapeutic pages 31-33, nalkiran2024cdc20andccnb1 pages 15-16)

Table: A concise evidence-weighted map of human CDC20 identity, molecular mechanism, localization, recent mechanistic advances and translational status. It distinguishes established APC/C biology from emerging disease associations and preclinical therapeutic proposals.

1. Identity verification and domain architecture

1.1 Verification

The supplied identity is internally and externally consistent:

  • Gene/protein: CDC20; cell division cycle protein 20 homolog/p55CDC.
  • Organism: Homo sapiens.
  • UniProt accession: Q12834.
  • Family: CDC20/Fizzy WD-repeat family.
  • Architecture: an N-terminal regulatory segment and a C-terminal WD40 β-propeller.

Recent human-cell work maps the regulatory N terminus approximately to residues 1–173, containing BM1, KILR, C-box, KEN-box, and CRY-box elements, and the WD40 β-propeller to approximately residues 174–470. The propeller contains substrate-degron-binding surfaces and APC/C-recognition determinants, directly aligning with the supplied InterPro WD40 and CDC20/Fizzy annotations (zhang2024functionalanalysisof pages 1-2).

This report does not conflate CDC20 with CDC20B or FZR1/CDH1. FZR1 is a related WD40 APC/C coactivator that predominates later in mitosis and G1, whereas CDC20 controls early mitotic APC/C activity and is the direct target of the spindle checkpoint (OpenTargets Search: -CDC20, yamano2019apcccurrentunderstanding pages 3-5).

2. Primary molecular function

2.1 CDC20 is an adaptor and coactivator, not a catalytic enzyme

APC/C contains the catalytic APC2–APC11 cullin–RING module. ATP-dependent E1 first activates ubiquitin; an E2 carries activated ubiquitin; APC/C positions E2~ubiquitin and the recruited substrate for ubiquitin transfer. CDC20 supplies substrate recognition and helps convert APC/C into its active mitotic conformation—it does not itself catalyze a standalone reaction (yamano2019apcccurrentunderstanding pages 3-5, liu2020theinteractionprofile pages 12-16, yamano2019apcccurrentunderstanding pages 1-3).

The principal biochemical reaction can therefore be written conceptually as:

APC/C–CDC20 substrate + E2~ubiquitin → polyubiquitylated substrate → 26S-proteasomal degradation.

UBE2C/UbcH10 or UBE2D/UbcH5 initiates substrate ubiquitylation, whereas UBE2S extends predominantly K11-linked chains; mixed or branched K11/K48 chains can also occur. CDC20's N-terminal C-box and C-terminal IR-tail help dock and activate APC/C, while its WD40 propeller recognizes substrate degrons (yamano2019apcccurrentunderstanding pages 3-5, yamano2019apcccurrentunderstanding pages 5-6).

2.2 Substrate specificity

CDC20 recognizes combinations of short linear degradation motifs rather than one chemically defined small-molecule substrate:

  • D-box: commonly summarized as RxxLxxxxN.
  • KEN-box: KENxxxN consensus in the cited synthesis.
  • ABBA motif: Fx[ILV][FY]x[DE].

D-box and KEN-box motifs engage distinct surfaces of the CDC20 WD40 propeller. Motif multiplicity, affinity, accessibility, nearby phosphorylation, APC10 participation, and competition by checkpoint proteins determine whether and when a substrate is ubiquitylated. Human structural work supports multisite degron recognition rather than a single lock-and-key site (cosma2022biochemicalbiophysicaland pages 14-15, cosma2022biochemicalbiophysicaland pages 13-14, yamano2019apcccurrentunderstanding pages 5-6).

2.3 Principal physiological substrates and consequences

The decisive metaphase–anaphase substrates are:

  1. Securin/PTTG1. Its degradation releases separase, which cleaves cohesin and permits sister-chromatid separation.
  2. Cyclin B1/CCNB1. Its degradation inactivates cyclin-B–CDK1, allowing mitotic exit.

Earlier APC/C–CDC20 substrates include cyclin A and Nek2A, whose destruction can proceed in prometaphase under regulatory rules that differ from the checkpoint-sensitive destruction of securin and cyclin B1. CDC20 dominates from prometaphase through metaphase/anaphase; CDH1/FZR1 assumes broader APC/C substrate control in late anaphase and G1 (yamano2019apcccurrentunderstanding pages 3-5, liu2020theinteractionprofile pages 44-47, liu2020theinteractionprofile pages 1-8).

3. Spindle-assembly-checkpoint pathway

CDC20 has an unusual dual role. When chromosomes have achieved correct bipolar attachment, CDC20 activates APC/C. When an unattached or improperly attached kinetochore persists, checkpoint signaling generates the MCC, comprising CDC20, MAD2, BUBR1, and BUB3. The MCC binds APC/C–CDC20 and blocks productive degron recognition and ligase activation. Structural and biochemical models contain two CDC20 molecules: one associated with APC/C and another incorporated into MCC. This arrangement prevents premature securin and cyclin-B destruction (cosma2022biochemicalbiophysicaland pages 8-10).

Mitotic CDK1–cyclin-B–Cks phosphorylation of APC/C subunits facilitates CDC20 recruitment, but checkpoint-generated MCC restrains the resulting ligase until attachment is satisfactory. Checkpoint silencing then permits APC/C–CDC20 to cross the irreversible anaphase threshold. This explains why CDC20 depletion or failure to engage APC/C produces metaphase arrest, whereas defective MCC engagement can cause premature anaphase and chromosome mis-segregation (yamano2019apcccurrentunderstanding pages 5-6, zhang2024functionalanalysisof pages 1-2).

4. Cellular localization and site of action

CDC20 carries out its canonical function in the mitotic chromosome–spindle/APC/C compartment, particularly at or downstream of unattached kinetochores and on soluble or spatially organized APC/C assemblies after nuclear-envelope breakdown. A simple assignment as exclusively “nuclear” or “cytoplasmic” is misleading because mitosis dismantles the nuclear envelope and redistributes these components.

Direct human-cell evidence is strongest for two spatial features:

  • In 2024 mutant-complementation experiments, tested CDC20 constructs localized to kinetochores except a mutant lacking the ABBA-binding region, indicating a requirement for that interface in kinetochore recruitment (zhang2024functionalanalysisof pages 3-5).
  • Endogenous HeLa-cell proximity assays found APC8–CDC20 proximity highest in prometaphase and APC3–CDC20 proximity highest in metaphase, supporting temporally reorganized APC/C engagement (liu2020theinteractionprofile pages 47-50).

Claims that human CDC20 stably decorates spindle microtubules should be made more cautiously. Some direct spindle-localization evidence in the retrieved literature concerns plant CDC20, whereas human structural and interaction studies primarily establish kinetochore/MCC/APC/C engagement. Broad nuclear-versus-cytosolic partitioning also remains less precisely resolved than CDC20's mitotic interaction geography (liu2020theinteractionprofile pages 1-8, cosma2022biochemicalbiophysicaland pages 10-11).

5. Biological processes and physiological significance

The core biological processes are:

  • spindle-assembly-checkpoint signaling;
  • APC/C-dependent protein ubiquitylation;
  • metaphase-to-anaphase transition;
  • sister-chromatid separation;
  • CDK1 inactivation and mitotic exit;
  • maintenance of chromosome number and genome stability;
  • analogous control of meiotic progression in oocytes.

The functional logic is highly constrained: too little APC/C–CDC20 activity causes prolonged metaphase arrest and often mitotic death; activity released too early permits segregation before correct attachment, producing aneuploidy. In human CRISPR-engineered cells, acute removal of residual CDC20 caused prolonged metaphase-to-anaphase timing, inefficient cyclin-B1 degradation, metaphase arrest, and cell death; RNAi-resistant CDC20 rescued the phenotype (zhang2024functionalanalysisof pages 1-2).

Human genetics supports an important reproductive role. Open Targets links CDC20 with female infertility due to oocyte meiotic arrest and oocyte maturation defect 14, consistent with the requirement for APC/C–CDC20 during meiotic chromosome segregation (OpenTargets Search: -CDC20). These genetic associations are stronger evidence of organism-level necessity than cancer-expression correlations, which can partly reflect the high mitotic fraction of tumors.

6. Important 2023–2024 developments

6.1 The CRY box is a checkpoint-signaling element

The most precise recent mechanistic advance is Zhang et al., published in Communications Biology in September 2024, DOI 10.1038/s42003-024-05859-6. Using CRISPR-engineered, RNAi-sensitive HeLa cells complemented with mutant CDC20 proteins, live-cell analysis, immunoprecipitation, and reconstituted ubiquitylation assays, the authors showed that the conserved CRY box is required for efficient MCC assembly and stable MCC association with APC/C. It is therefore not merely a CDC20 degradation signal (zhang2024functionalanalysisof pages 11-11, zhang2024functionalanalysisof pages 1-2, zhang2024functionalanalysisof pages 7-8).

The biochemical assay used 50 nM APC/C, 50 nM CDC20, 200 nM cyclin B1, 30 nM UBA1, 20 μM ubiquitin, 5 mM ATP, 250 nM UbcH10, and 270 nM MCC constructs for 15 minutes at 25°C. These conditions provide direct mechanistic testing of how CRY-box or BUBR1-interface mutations change MCC inhibition of APC/C–CDC20-dependent cyclin-B1 ubiquitylation (zhang2024functionalanalysisof pages 11-11).

6.2 Structural refinement of APC/C regulation

High-resolution human APC/C cryo-EM work in 2023–2024 refined APC/C architecture and coactivator-dependent regulation, although the retrieved new structures emphasized apo-APC/C and APC/C–CDH1–EMI1 rather than a new CDC20 complex. They reinforce that CDC20 and CDH1 are sequential, structurally related coactivators whose N-terminal contacts are not identical. This is an evolving structural framework rather than a revision of CDC20's established primary function.

7. Disease relevance and recent quantitative studies

7.1 Cancer: strong association, emerging causality, no clinical validation yet

CDC20 is commonly elevated in proliferative tumors. Mechanistically this is plausible because excess or mistimed CDC20 can weaken checkpoint restraint, accelerate mitotic progression, or support survival of chromosome-unstable cells. Nevertheless, CDC20 expression also tracks cell proliferation; therefore, expression–outcome associations alone do not prove that CDC20 is an independent oncogenic driver.

Breast cancer. A July 2024 Cancers analysis, DOI 10.3390/cancers16142546, integrated TCGA and several public resources. It reported tumor-associated CDC20 elevation, adverse clinical associations, and a median survival of 63.6 months in the high-expression group versus 169.2 months in the low-expression group. Preclinical evidence summarized in the same study found that CDC20 deficiency reduced growth and metastatic phenotypes, induced G2/M arrest, and increased paclitaxel cytotoxicity. However, some TNBC subgroup analyses did not find significant DMFS, OS, or DFS differences, illustrating cohort and endpoint dependence (messeha2024prognosticandtherapeutic pages 31-33, messeha2024prognosticandtherapeutic pages 29-31, messeha2024prognosticandtherapeutic pages 2-4).

Lung adenocarcinoma. Miao et al., Frontiers in Oncology, July 2024, DOI 10.3389/fonc.2024.1351393, analyzed 279 samples from GSE166722 and GSE31210, identified 91 recurrence/invasion/stemness-associated differentially expressed genes, and selected CDC45, CDC20, and TPX2 by machine-learning methods. CDC20 knockdown reduced migration, invasion, proliferation, cell-cycle progression, and mouse xenograft growth, potentially involving MAPK signaling. This combines retrospective biomarker discovery with preclinical functional validation, but it is not a prospective clinical test (miao2024basedonmachine pages 7-11).

Bladder cancer. Nalkiran et al., published online in December 2024 in Diagnostics 15:59, DOI 10.3390/diagnostics15010059, studied 54 tumors: 23 low-grade and 31 high-grade. Mean CDC20 fold change was 16.1 in high-grade versus 10.54 in low-grade tumors (p=0.039), with a grade correlation of r=0.284, p=0.038. CDC20 did not significantly associate with stage or overall survival in that cohort. This is a useful counterexample to overgeneralized claims that high CDC20 always predicts survival (nalkiran2024cdc20andccnb1 pages 15-16).

Cervical cancer. A November 2024 preprint examined 249 patients and reported CDC20 positivity in 61.85% of tumors versus 7.5% of adjacent tissues. Its xenograft study reported tumor volumes of 10.42 mm³ for control, 8.12 mm³ for Apcin, 6.21 mm³ for cisplatin, and 3.2 mm³ for the combination, with p<0.01. These unusually small reported volumes and the preprint status warrant cautious interpretation and independent validation (xian2024prognosticimpactand pages 14-17, xian2024prognosticimpactand pages 11-14).

7.2 Emerging noncanonical cancer mechanism

A 2024 study proposed a DNA-repair role in which nuclear CDC20 initially stabilizes RPA1 and supports ATR signaling, then later promotes RPA1 ubiquitylation and degradation, thereby contributing to homologous-recombination repair and radio/chemoresistance. This work used tumor cell lines and xenografts. It is intriguing but should be regarded as an emerging context-specific extension, not a replacement for the canonical APC/C–mitosis function.

8. Applications and real-world implementation

8.1 Current applications

Current practical use is predominantly research-oriented:

  • CDC20 transcript or protein is evaluated in retrospective tumor cohorts as a proliferation/prognostic biomarker.
  • CDC20 and MCC components are manipulated experimentally to study checkpoint fidelity, aneuploidy, drug sensitivity, and mitotic death.
  • Human CDC20 variants can be considered in genetic investigation of recurrent oocyte maturation arrest or early embryonic failure, supported by disease-target and human genetic evidence (OpenTargets Search: -CDC20).

CDC20 immunostaining or expression profiling is not established as a universal standard-of-care diagnostic or companion biomarker. Cancer results vary by tumor type, cohort, endpoint, and adjustment for proliferation and stage.

8.2 Therapeutic strategies under investigation

Proposed strategies include:

  • Apcin, which interferes with CDC20-dependent degron recognition;
  • TAME/proTAME, APC/C inhibitors that mimic the coactivator IR motif and destabilize productive APC/C–coactivator engagement;
  • CDC20 knockdown or genetic suppression;
  • combining partial APC/C–CDC20 suppression with paclitaxel, cisplatin, radiation, KIF18A inhibition, or other spindle-directed treatments.

The mechanistic aim is generally to delay or block anaphase, increase mitotic stress, or prevent survival after abnormal spindle assembly. However, complete CDC20 suppression also threatens normal proliferating tissues and genome stability, creating a narrow therapeutic window. Selectivity, dosing, and whether tumor cells undergo durable death rather than mitotic slippage remain central unresolved issues (nalkiran2024cdc20andccnb1 pages 15-16, xian2024prognosticimpactand pages 11-14).

The clinical-trial search retrieved no relevant CDC20-directed interventional trial, and the literature reviewed here reports no approved CDC20-specific therapy. Thus, descriptions of CDC20 as “druggable” or “therapeutic” currently refer to preclinical potential, not established clinical implementation.

9. Expert interpretation and evidence-weighted conclusions

  1. The canonical annotation is exceptionally strong. Human CDC20 Q12834 is a conserved CDC20/Fizzy-family WD40 protein whose primary role is APC/C activation and substrate recruitment. Its exact function is not generic “cell-cycle regulation” but temporal selection of proteins for APC/C-mediated polyubiquitylation at the metaphase–anaphase transition (yamano2019apcccurrentunderstanding pages 5-6, yamano2019apcccurrentunderstanding pages 3-5).

  2. CDC20 is both the accelerator and the checkpoint target. The same molecule that activates APC/C is captured by MAD2–BUBR1–BUB3 when attachment is incomplete. This dual use creates a rapid, switch-like coupling between kinetochore status and anaphase (cosma2022biochemicalbiophysicaland pages 8-10).

  3. Localization is dynamic and interaction-defined. The best-supported human localization is at kinetochores and in temporally changing APC/C/MCC assemblies during mitosis. Assertions of fixed nuclear, cytoplasmic, centrosomal, or spindle-wide localization should be qualified by cell-cycle stage and experimental system (zhang2024functionalanalysisof pages 3-5, liu2020theinteractionprofile pages 47-50).

  4. The 2024 CRY-box result materially improves annotation. It assigns a positive checkpoint-signaling function to a motif previously discussed principally as a degron: the CRY box stabilizes MCC formation and APC/C inhibition (zhang2024functionalanalysisof pages 1-2, zhang2024functionalanalysisof pages 7-8).

  5. Human disease evidence is uneven. Biallelic loss-of-function and oocyte maturation arrest directly fit the canonical meiotic function. Cancer overexpression is widespread and experimentally actionable, but many clinical claims remain retrospective and confounded by proliferation. The bladder study's absence of an overall-survival association underscores the need for tumor-specific validation (OpenTargets Search: -CDC20, nalkiran2024cdc20andccnb1 pages 15-16).

  • Zhang Y. et al. “Functional analysis of Cdc20 reveals a critical role of CRY box in mitotic checkpoint signaling.” Communications Biology, September 2024. https://doi.org/10.1038/s42003-024-05859-6 (zhang2024functionalanalysisof pages 11-11).
  • Messeha S.S. et al. “Prognostic and Therapeutic Implications of Cell Division Cycle 20 Homolog in Breast Cancer.” Cancers, July 2024. https://doi.org/10.3390/cancers16142546 (messeha2024prognosticandtherapeutic pages 31-33).
  • Miao R. et al. “Based on machine learning, CDC20 has been identified as a biomarker for postoperative recurrence and progression in stage I & II lung adenocarcinoma patients.” Frontiers in Oncology, July 2024. https://doi.org/10.3389/fonc.2024.1351393 (miao2024basedonmachine pages 7-11).
  • Nalkiran H.S. et al. “CDC20 and CCNB1 Overexpression as Prognostic Markers in Bladder Cancer.” Published December 2024, Diagnostics 15:59. https://doi.org/10.3390/diagnostics15010059 (nalkiran2024cdc20andccnb1 pages 15-16).
  • Yamano H. “APC/C: current understanding and future perspectives.” F1000Research, May 2019. https://doi.org/10.12688/f1000research.18582.1 (yamano2019apcccurrentunderstanding pages 5-6, yamano2019apcccurrentunderstanding pages 3-5).
  • Tian W. et al. “Structural analysis of human Cdc20 supports multisite degron recognition by APC/C.” PNAS, 2012. https://doi.org/10.1073/pnas.1213438109 (cosma2022biochemicalbiophysicaland pages 14-15).

Overall, the appropriate functional annotation is: human CDC20 is a dynamic mitotic WD40 adaptor that activates APC/C and recognizes degron-bearing substrates, while also serving as the central inhibited target of the spindle-assembly checkpoint. Its principal cellular site of action is the kinetochore–MCC–APC/C regulatory network during mitosis and meiosis. Cancer biomarker and inhibitor applications are promising but remain largely retrospective or preclinical.

📚 Additional Documentation

Notes

(CDC20-notes.md)

CDC20 (human, UniProt Q12834) — curation notes

Working journal for the GO annotation review of human CDC20 (p55CDC), the WD40
coactivator of the anaphase-promoting complex/cyclosome (APC/C).

Identity

  • HGNC symbol CDC20; UniProt Q12834; "Cell division cycle protein 20 homolog", alias p55CDC.
  • Distinct from the paralogous coactivator FZR1/CDH1 and from CDC20B.
  • Domain architecture (UniProt features): N-terminal regulatory region with the C-box,
    KEN box, CRY box, MAD2-binding segment and KILR motif; C-terminal WD40 beta-propeller
    (WD1 starts at residue 173) and the C-terminal IR tail.
  • First described as p55CDC, a G-beta-repeat protein of cycling mammalian cells
    PMID:7513050.

Core biology (synthesis)

  1. APC/C coactivator and substrate adaptor. CDC20 is not an enzyme. It binds the
    phosphorylated APC/C through its C-box (to APC8/CDC23) and IR tail (to APC3/CDC27)
    and stimulates the ligase; its WD40 propeller binds D-box, KEN-box and ABBA degrons
    on substrates [PMID:27120157 "Coactivators recognize substrate degrons, and enhance the affinity of the APC/C for its cognate E2"; PMID:27509861 "Cdc20, the APC/C coactivator subunit responsible for substrate interactions"].
    APC/C activation by CDC20 requires mitotic hyperphosphorylation of APC/C, which
    displaces an Apc1 auto-inhibitory segment from the C-box binding site
    PMID:27120157.
  2. Metaphase-to-anaphase transition. APC/C-CDC20 ubiquitinates securin and cyclin B1;
    securin loss activates separase, cyclin B1 loss inactivates CDK1. The ternary
    MAD2-CDC20-APC complex is inactive at metaphase and the CDC20-APC binary complex is
    active at anaphase PMID:9637688.
    Additional CDC20-dependent substrates documented in the cited literature: NEK2A and
    KIF18A PMID:23288039, BUBR1 after acetylation-switch PMID:19407811,
    USP22 PMID:27030811,
    FBXO31 PMID:29343641,
    conductin/AXIN2 PMID:22322943,
    FBXW5 PMID:21725316.
  3. Target of the spindle assembly checkpoint (SAC). Unattached kinetochores catalyse
    incorporation of CDC20 into the mitotic checkpoint complex (MCC = BUBR1-BUB3-CDC20-MAD2)
    PMID:11535616.
    MAD2 binds a 40-residue segment N-terminal to the WD40 repeats
    PMID:10700282.
    In the APC/C-MCC structure, BUBR1 degron-like motifs block the degron receptor sites
    on CDC20 and obstruct UbcH10 binding PMID:27509861.
    The MCC also inhibits a second, APC/C-bound CDC20 PMID:25383541.
    Checkpoint silencing involves APC/C-driven ubiquitination of the MCC-intrinsic CDC20
    PMID:17443186 counteracted by USP44
    PMID:17443180,
    an ATP-dependent step PMID:20212161 promoted by p31comet PMID:21300909.
    BUB1 phosphorylation of CDC20 inhibits APC/C-CDC20 catalytically PMID:15525512.
  4. Meiosis I. Biallelic CDC20 mutations cause oocyte maturation arrest (MI arrest),
    fertilization failure and early embryonic arrest (OZEMA14)
    [PMID:32666501 "we identified biallelic CDC20 mutations in five infertile individuals with oocyte maturation arrest, fertilization failure, and early embryonic arrest"; "In oocytes, the activation of APC/C by CDC20 is a key step in homologue disjunction and in transition from meiosis I to meiosis II"].
  5. Localization. Kinetochores (GFP-Cdc20 turns over rapidly at unattached kinetochores
    PMID:15182668; human CDC20 localizes to kinetochores in mouse oocytes and truncating
    mutants fail to PMID:32666501),
    centrosome/spindle pole (UniProt, PubMed:20034488, not cached), and the mitotic cytosol
    where APC/C-CDC20 and MCC act (Reactome). Nucleoplasm rows come from Reactome
    reactions involving EMI1/FBXO5 and USP44.
  6. Regulation. SIRT2 deacetylates CDC20 (K66), enhancing CDC27 binding
    PMID:22014574.
    Inhibitors: EMI1/FBXO5 PMID:11988738, RASSF1A PMID:14743218,
    MAD2B/MAD2L2 PMID:11459826.
  7. Neuronal roles (rodent, by similarity). A centrosomal Cdc20-APC pathway regulates
    dendrite morphogenesis and presynaptic differentiation in rodent neurons (UniProt "By
    similarity" statements; GOA rows are ISS/IEA from mouse/rat). Not demonstrated in human;
    graded non-core.

Curation decisions (rationale summary)

  • Protein binding (67 IPI rows). Following the repository policy, none kept as bare
    GO:0005515. Partner classes:
  • CDC27/ANAPC3 and ANAPC4 partners from mechanistic papers -> MODIFY to GO:0010997
    anaphase-promoting complex binding (the IR tail/C-box contacts with APC3/APC8 are the
    structural basis of coactivator binding).
  • MAD2L1 partners from papers on the MAD2-CDC20 interaction -> MODIFY to GO:1990333
    mitotic checkpoint complex, CDC20-MAD2 subcomplex (consistent with the MAD2L1 review).
  • MAD2L1/BUB1B partners from whole-MCC papers -> MODIFY to GO:0033597 mitotic
    checkpoint complex.
  • Partners that are APC/C-CDC20 substrates (BUBR1 after acetylation, AXIN2, FBXW5) ->
    MODIFY to GO:1990756 ubiquitin-like ligase-substrate adaptor activity.
  • SIRT2 -> MODIFY to GO:0042826 histone deacetylase binding (anchors the rat-derived IEA).
  • High-throughput interactome rows (MitoCheck, BioPlex, OpenCell, Clone-seq, kinase
    network, cell maps, PLA screen, TRAIL kinase study), inhibitor-binding rows (EMI1,
    RASSF1A, MAD2L2, IpaB study), regulator rows (USP44, HSF1, CDK5RAP2, CCNF) and the
    stPEPC drug study -> REMOVE as uninformative; removal does not dispute the interaction.
  • GO:0005819 spindle TAS (PMID:7513050) kept non-core: the 1994 source only infers a
    spindle role from yeast homology; later work supports spindle-pole/kinetochore
    association.
  • GO:0044784 (generic metaphase/anaphase transition) -> MODIFY to the mitotic child
    GO:0007091; the meiotic child GO:1990949 already carries IMP.
  • GO:0007346 regulation of mitotic cell cycle (NAS, review) -> MODIFY to
    GO:0045842, the specific IDA-supported process.
  • GO:0051445 regulation of meiotic cell cycle (NAS, review) -> MODIFY to GO:1990949.
  • GO:0008284 positive regulation of cell population proliferation (IEA from rat) ->
    MARK_AS_OVER_ANNOTATED (downstream consequence of being required for mitosis).
  • Neuronal terms (GO:0031915, GO:0090129, GO:0050773) -> non-core / over-annotated;
    rodent-only, "By similarity".
  • GO:0032991 protein-containing complex (IEA) -> MODIFY to the specific complexes.
  • No NEW terms proposed: GO-CAMs 6348a65d00002236 and 657ba49f00001888 and the module
    metaphase_anaphase_transition_and_mitotic_exit already place CDC20 under GO:0010997 /
    GO:1990756 / GO:1990757, GO:0045842 and GO:0007094, which the existing GOA covers.

📄 View Raw YAML

 
id: Q12834
gene_symbol: CDC20
product_type: PROTEIN
status: COMPLETE
taxon:
  id: NCBITaxon:9606
  label: Homo sapiens
description: >-
  CDC20 (p55CDC) is the mitotic coactivator of the anaphase-promoting complex/cyclosome (APC/C), the multisubunit
  cullin-RING E3 ubiquitin ligase that drives the metaphase-to-anaphase transition and mitotic exit. CDC20 has no
  catalytic activity of its own: its N-terminal C-box, KILR motif and C-terminal IR tail dock it onto the mitotically
  phosphorylated APC/C (APC8/CDC23 and APC3/CDC27), where it relieves APC1 auto-inhibition, enhances E2 recruitment
  and, through its C-terminal WD40 beta-propeller, recognises D-box, KEN-box and ABBA degrons on substrates. APC/C-CDC20
  polyubiquitinates securin (PTTG1) and cyclin B1, releasing separase to cleave cohesin and inactivating CDK1, and
  also targets cyclin A, NEK2A, KIF18A, BUBR1, USP22, FBXO31 and AXIN2. CDC20 is the direct target of the spindle
  assembly checkpoint: unattached kinetochores catalyse its incorporation into the mitotic checkpoint complex (BUBR1-BUB3-CDC20-MAD2),
  in which MAD2 and BUBR1 occupy its degron receptor sites and block E2 access so that APC/C stays inactive until
  all chromosomes are bioriented; APC/C-mediated ubiquitination of MCC-bound CDC20, opposed by USP44 and promoted
  by p31comet, then dissolves the MCC and reactivates the ligase. CDC20 is synthesised from G1/S, peaks in mitosis,
  is regulated by BUB1 phosphorylation and SIRT2 deacetylation, and is degraded at the M/G1 transition when CDH1/FZR1
  takes over APC/C activation. It localises to kinetochores, spindle poles/centrosomes and the mitotic cytosol.
  In oocytes, APC/C-CDC20 executes homologue disjunction at the meiosis I metaphase/anaphase transition, and biallelic
  CDC20 loss-of-function variants cause oocyte maturation arrest and female infertility (OZEMA14). In rodent postmitotic
  neurons a centrosomal Cdc20-APC pool additionally regulates dendrite morphogenesis and presynaptic differentiation.

    action: ACCEPT
    reason: >-

      human CDC20 constructs localising to kinetochores in oocytes (PMID:32666501), by live-cell FRAP at unattached
      kinetochores (PMID:15182668) and by UniProt subcellular location. Accepted as a core location.

      the APC/C-MCC assembly) and is already captured by the specific complex and APC/C-binding annotations. A bare
      protein-binding row from a screen adds no functional information, so it is removed; removal does not mean
      the interaction is false.
    supported_by:
    - reference_id: PMID:35271311
      supporting_text: >-
        We combined genome engineering, confocal live-cell imaging, mass spectrometry, and data science to systematically
        map the localization and interactions of human proteins

  review:
    summary: >-
      CDC20 is the mitotic coactivator subunit of the APC/C: it docks on the phosphorylated APC/C through its C-box

      in which APC/C-CDC20 or the MCC-bound CDC20 participates directly.
    action: ACCEPT
    reason: >-
      After nuclear-envelope breakdown the APC/C-CDC20 ligase and the MCC act in the mitotic cytosol, where securin,
      cyclin B1, cyclin A and NEK2A are ubiquitinated and where MCC assembly and disassembly occur. Cytosol is therefore
      the compartment of the core function and the annotation is accepted.

core_functions:
- description: >-
    Ubiquitin ligase activator of the anaphase-promoting complex/cyclosome: CDC20 docks on the mitotically phosphorylated
    APC/C via its C-box, KILR motif and IR tail, relieves Apc1 auto-inhibition and enhances E2 affinity, converting
    the APC/C into the active APC/C-CDC20 ligase that polyubiquitinates securin and cyclin B1 to trigger sister-chromatid
    separation and CDK1 inactivation at the metaphase/anaphase transition.
  molecular_function:
    id: GO:1990757
    label: ubiquitin ligase activator activity

- description: >-
    Substrate adaptor of the APC/C: the CDC20 WD40 beta-propeller recognises D-box, KEN-box and ABBA degrons on
    substrates (securin, cyclin B1, cyclin A, NEK2A, KIF18A, BUBR1, USP22, FBXO31, AXIN2) and presents them to the
    APC2-APC11 catalytic module for polyubiquitination and proteasomal degradation.

- description: >-
    Inhibited effector of the spindle assembly checkpoint: at unattached kinetochores CDC20 is captured by C-MAD2

    can also inhibit a second APC/C-bound CDC20, holding APC/C inactive until chromosome biorientation; APC/C-mediated
    ubiquitination of MCC-bound CDC20 then reactivates the ligase.

suggested_questions:
- question: >-
    Should GO represent CDC20's two coactivator facets with both GO:1990757 (ubiquitin ligase activator activity)
    and GO:1990756 (ubiquitin-like ligase-substrate adaptor activity), as the current IBA and IDA rows do, or is
    one term preferred for APC/C coactivators, given that the human GO-CAMs use GO:0010997 for the same node?
- question: >-
    Which molecular-function or component term best represents CDC20 as the inhibited target of the spindle assembly
    checkpoint, given that MCC membership (GO:0033597, GO:1990333) is the only informative replacement for the many
    MAD2L1/BUB1B protein-binding rows?
- question: >-
    Should the rodent neuronal Cdc20-APC roles (dendrite morphogenesis, presynaptic differentiation, synapse maturation)
    remain on human CDC20 as ISS/IEA transfers, given the absence of human evidence and their taxon-restricted physiological
    context?
suggested_experiments:
- hypothesis: >-
    The CDC20 CRY box and ABBA-binding surface are required for MCC assembly and kinetochore recruitment independently
    of CDC20's own degradation.
  description: >-
    Complement CRISPR-engineered RNAi-sensitive human cells with CRY-box, ABBA-site and BUBR1-interface CDC20 mutants;
    quantify MCC formation by co-IP, kinetochore localisation by live imaging, and APC/C-CDC20 inhibition in reconstituted
    cyclin B1 ubiquitination assays.
  experiment_type: mutational rescue and reconstituted ubiquitination assay
- hypothesis: >-
    Human OZEMA14 missense variants (p.Tyr228Cys, p.Arg322Gln, p.Leu439Arg) impair APC/C activation or degron recognition
    rather than kinetochore localisation.
  description: >-
    Purify recombinant variant CDC20 proteins and compare APC/C binding, securin/cyclin B1 ubiquitination kinetics
    and MCC incorporation with wild type; complement Cdc20-depleted mouse oocytes and score meiosis I timing.
  experiment_type: biochemical reconstitution and oocyte complementation

- id: file:human/CDC20/CDC20-deep-research-falcon.md
  title: Deep research report for human CDC20 (falcon / Edison Scientific Literature)
  findings:
  - statement: >-
      CDC20 is a non-enzymatic WD40 coactivator and substrate receptor of the APC/C; APC/C, not CDC20, is the E3
      ligase.
  - statement: >-
      CDC20 is both the APC/C activator after correct attachment and the inhibited target sequestered in the MCC
      while kinetochores are unattached.
